I accidentally drank poop water for a week (vanlife in Arkansas)

published 2026-05-03 · in the recent-cohort window the advice is computed over · analysed 2026-09-01

1 · Retention breakdown

curve100 samples, stored one per percent runtime23:02 gone by 0:3045.5% at halfway30% still watching methodhazard = (present before − present after) ÷ present before at each moment; baseline = mean hazard of the previous 5 moments, floored; flagged when the excess viewers lost beyond baseline ranks high; a rise is a rewind only when no comparable drop precedes it and the lift survives the next sample

Red marks are flagged drops, green are earned rewinds. Hover a mark for its note.

2 · Each flagged moment: from curve to note

drop0:00
1 · what the curve showedTwo-thirds of the audience is gone by the 15-second mark (*66.1% remaining vs the channel's 80.4% median, beating only 2 of 59 other videos*). 2 · evidence at that secondno transcript line available; the note uses check-this-timestamp language 3 · why (the check or the quote)Check 0:00-0:14 for a cold open that does not visually deliver the thumbnail's poop-water promise before viewers bail. 4 · how (the next move)Test opening on the on-camera moment she is told the lake is contaminated with feces, then cut to the week-in-vanlife setup. 5 · rationaleThe channel's own intros hold 14.2 points more audience here, so the lever is a stronger promise-payoff, not a shorter runtime.
drop8:59
1 · what the curve showedA 55-second stretch loses 303 viewers at nearly ten times the surrounding stop rate (*9.9x spike, 5.2x the channel norm*). 2 · evidence at that second“started wrapping them and selling them” (transcript) 3 · why (the check or the quote)She pivots off the socket repair into a merch and guides pitch: "started wrapping them and selling them to my followers." 4 · how (the next move)Cut 8:52-9:30 and rejoin on the shark-teeth or crystal foraging b-roll without the selling and guides aside. 5 · rationaleThe channel typically loses 0.81% here and this video loses 4.21%, so the self-promo aside is the specific thing bleeding audience. cautionIf the merch mention is a contractual or revenue commitment, trial the cut on a re-upload and keep it if end-audience holds.
peak10:50
1 · what the curve showedAudience rises 12.1% and holds for 51 samples, regaining 165 viewers (*29.2% present, the video's strongest sustained rewind*). 2 · evidence at that second“contaminated with um feces” (transcript) 3 · why (the check or the quote)Viewers scrub back to the contamination reveal: "the water quality is actually bad and potentially might be contaminated with um feces." 4 · how (the next move)Front-load a 6-8 second tease of this reveal into the first 30 seconds so the promise lands before viewers bail at 0:15. 5 · rationaleThis is the video's title moment and viewers are rewinding to it 47% in, so surfacing it early converts the opening loss into hold.
peak14:17
1 · what the curve showedAudience rises 4.9% and holds for 10 samples, regaining 74 viewers on the stuck-zipper struggle (*30.6% present*). 2 · evidence at that second“literally bleeding. But I got it” (transcript) 3 · why (the check or the quote)Viewers replay the small-stakes victory: "I've been trying for hours and I'm literally bleeding. But I got it." 4 · how (the next move)Show the bleeding-hands payoff on-screen and keep this beat intact in future edits rather than trimming it for pace. 5 · rationaleA held rewind on a minor struggle-payoff signals this audience wants the process moments, not just the vanlife scenery.
peak15:40
1 · what the curve showedAudience rises 8.6% for 4 samples, regaining 134 viewers on the fishing beat (*32.2% present at 68% through*). 2 · evidence at that second“None of them want my worm” (transcript) 3 · why (the check or the quote)Viewers rewind to the terse fishing-and-coffee rhythm: "Fish are jumping. None of them want my worm." 4 · how (the next move)Show more of these short observational beats between longer segments, modelled on the 15:11-15:25 cadence. 5 · rationaleThe rewind proves the sparse, dry-humor cadence is what this audience returns to, so leaning into it should hold the mid-video.
drop15:54
1 · what the curve showedA 14-second window sheds 137 viewers at 3.5x the channel norm (*5.4x the surrounding stop rate, 34.3% down to 31.0%*). 2 · evidence at that secondno transcript line available; the note uses check-this-timestamp language 3 · why (the check or the quote)Check 15:54-16:07 for a lull right after the coffee beat where nothing new is introduced before the fishing attempt resumes. 4 · how (the next move)Tighten 15:54-16:07 to two seconds of ambient b-roll and cut straight to the next fishing or foraging action. 5 · rationaleChannel's typical stop rate here is 0.73% versus this video's 2.55%, so a dead beat is costing viewers the channel usually keeps.

drop0:00

drop7:04
1 · what the curve showedA 24-second window sheds 290 viewers at 6.1x the surrounding stop rate, pulling audience from 29.0% to 26.4% (*3.5x the channel norm here*). 2 · evidence at that second“Time to look for rock” (transcript) 3 · why (the check or the quote)The narration hands off to 30 seconds of music after "time to look for rock" — a rock-hunting errand with no stakes tied to the rafting or kitten premise. 4 · how (the next move)Cut 7:02-7:32 of music-only b-roll and rejoin on the next spoken beat, or replace with a 5-second line naming what was actually found. 5 · rationaleMusic-only stretches account for both mid-video hazard spikes, so trimming them protects the audience that has already survived the opening cliff.

So, let's go” (transcript) 3 · why (the check or the quote)Viewers return for the rock-hunting invitation — "they just called me over to go look for rocks" — a concrete activity after a stretch of static exposition about Perry. 4 · how (the next move)Tighten 6:36-6:55 by cutting the Perry sleeping-arrangement lines and jump into the rock hunt within 10 seconds of introducing it. 5 · rationaleThe rewind lands on the action cue, not the exposition, so compressing the setup delivers the moment viewers actually came back for.
